# Frontier records ledger

```text
Status: IMPORTED (literature records) with independent re-verification verdicts
Scope: classical zeta unless an entry says otherwise; search cutoff 2026-11-17
Exact sources or dependencies: per entry (source + location)
What was actually run: one drafting agent per area reading primary sources; one independent skeptic per area re-checking each entry
Smallest remaining gap: entries marked unverifiable / superseded / corrected below
```

Each entry gives the drafting agent's evidence level and the independent verifier's verdict. **Unrefereed and AI-generated claims are labelled as such; a verdict of `confirmed_fulltext` means the statement matches the source, not that the source's proof was checked.**


## ARITH: ARITH — Arithmetic equivalents of RH for the classical zeta function and their computational frontiers: Robin, Lagarias and Nicolas criteria; Mertens function M(x); Skewes number for π(x) − li(x); exp

**Verifier critique.** Overall: the records are mostly accurate and well sourced. I opened and checked every 'current-best' claim against the full text except Saouter–Trudgian–Demichel, for which I used the zbMATH review. Errors are concentrated in the drafter's own heuristics and wording, not in the quoted records.  (1) The t-free Robin bottleneck is mis-located and mis-quantified. - (a) The heuristic script uses log x where the criterion needs log θ(x). With the correct normalisation, Nicolas 2012 (under RH; numerically to 10^9) gives a true excess e^{-γ}∏p/(p−1)/log θ − 1 ≈ (1.24–2.28)/(√x log x) ≈ 0.7–1.3·10^{-8} at p_k0. The draft's 2–7·10^{-9} is too small. The perfect-information ceiling is therefore t ≈ 26, not 27, and the margin against 2^{-22} is about 20–30×, not 100×. - (b) The 'middle range [10^26, e^{10^4}]' is not binding. Published ε-type bounds there (Büthe 2016 as corrected by Bhattacharjee: 1.24·10^{-11} for x ≥ e^60; Chirre–Helfgott preprint: 1.05·10^{-12}) contribute ≲ 10^{-12} to the Mertens-product error. The binding region is x ≈ p_k0, where Axler's uniform closed-form product bound (relative error ≈ 3.05·10^{-7} at p_k0) is the actual obstruction for t = 22. - (c) The proposed next step would fail as stated. Johnston's RH-strength bound \|θ(x) − x\| < √x log²x/(8π), integrated in the Mertens-product formula, gives an error ≈ log x/(4π√x) ≈ 4.5·10^{-7} at p_k0. That exceeds 2^{-22} ≈ 2.4·10^{-7}, and it is the same mechanism that makes MP's g_B(·;21) > 1. One must instead use Büthe 2018's computed bounds (0.05√x < x − θ(x) ≤ 1.95√x on [1423, 10^19]). In my uncertified arithmetic, the boundary terms cancel against log θ(x) and the integral term gives a rigorous excess ≲ 3.9/(√x log x) ≈ 2.3·10^{-8}. That would in principle suffice up to t ≈ 25. If so, '25-free' is a near-term target rather than a breakthrough. This needs a careful explicit check.  (2) The Johnston–Trudgian 'locality barrier' is overstated. Their \|γ\| ≲ Y^{1/5} describes their explicit Landau–Pintz method with B_G = 7/2 ('approximately'). It is not a theorem bounding what any arithmetic verification can yield, and it concerns M(x), not Robin or Lagarias. The quoted numbers (β > 0.99, γ < 5 from 10^16; γ < 10^13 from 10^80; β > 0.51, γ < exp(10^16) from exp(10^19); 'doomed to fail') are all verbatim-correct.  (3) The explicit-PNT frontier omits the intermediate range. For x < e^{2394}, ε-type bounds dominate the de la Vallée Poussin-shape record: Chirre–Helfgott (arXiv 2512.15709, unrefereed) give \|ψ(x) − x\| ≤ πx/(3·10^12) + 113.67√x for all x ≥ 1, and 34.409x/log⁴x for x ≥ 69991. BFKNW confirm the e^{2394} crossover. The frontier also lists Chirre–Helfgott as a mere 'input'.  (4) Wording and numeric slips: - '×1300 in log10 log10 N' should read log10 N. - Breakthrough (iii): at p_max ≈ 2.3·10^15, ε ≈ 0.0094243/35.37³ ≈ 2.1·10^{-7}, not 2.4·10^{-7}. - ARITH-ROBIN-UNCOND misdescribes the exceptional set A.  (5) Negative results. These are real and correctly stated: Odlyzko–te Riele; Ingham under LI; Best–Trudgian (500 heaviest zeros, \|c_i\| ≤ 4976); Kotnik–van de Lune / Kaczorowski / Ng–Gonek exponents 1/2, 1, 5/4 (confirmed via Kim–Nguyen §4.2); Choie–Planat–Solé (Cramér); Broucke (abstract); and Rozmarynowycz–Kim's omission of StR14. On the Kim–Nguyen versus Kotnik–van de Lune point: the extrapolated location exp(5.15·10^23) is falsified, but their growth law is not refuted.  (6) Skewes. The upper record is unconditional and correct, but the draft omits RH-conditional sharpenings (STD 2015 RH version; Smith 2016 MSc, 1.397166161527·10^316) and an advertised, untraced Hughes–Smith–Platt improvement.  (7) Ingredients worth adding: - Hathi 2023's refinement of the Saouter–te Riele estimates (Kim–Nguyen §4.1) for the first Mertens counterexample. - The Tao-led formalization of explicit PNT papers (BKLNW, FKS, Chirre–Helfgott), which directly addresses the erratum-chain problem. - Wu's CA2 reduction (fewer than 1 in 1000 CA numbers) for any extension of the Robin range.

**Missing records flagged by verifier.** Intermediate-range explicit ψ bound. Chirre–Helfgott, arXiv 2512.15709 (Dec 2025, unrefereed), Cor 1.3: \|ψ(x) − x\| ≤ πx/(3·10^12) + 113.67√x for all x ≥ 1, better than FKS for x < e^{2394.19}; (10.3): \|ψ(x) − x\| ≤ 34.409x/log⁴x for x ≥ 69991. BFKNW 2026 Remark 8 confirms the comparison. A companion paper on M(x) (bounded functions) is announced.; ε-type \|ψ(x) − x\| ≤ εx table records for x ≥ e^60 and x ≥ e^100. Büthe 2016 as corrected by Bhattacharjee 2024: 1.23991·10^{-11} and 2.63677·10^{-12} at T = 2.445·10^12. Dusart 2018's 2.978·10^{-11} is affected by the Ramaré error. Chirre–Helfgott: 1.16840·10^{-11} and 1.04720·10^{-12} (CH Tables 1–2).; Explicit unconditional bounds on \|M(x)\|: Lee–Leong, arXiv 2208.06141 (shapes x log x exp(−η√log x) and Korobov–Vinogradov); the classical ε x and x/log x type bounds (Cohen–Dress–El Marraki; Ramaré). The older constants are from memory and need checking. The area scope names M(x), but the ledger has only oscillation and range records.; Wu, 'Subsets of colossally abundant numbers', arXiv 1903.03490 (2019 preprint), Cor. 4 as restated by Leshchiner–Musin–Thompson arXiv 2610.39120: RH ⟺ G(n) < e^γ for every CA2 number n > 5040. CA2 is fewer than 1/1000 of CA numbers in Wu's sample, which bears on any extension of the Robin range.; Luca–Solé, 'On Robin's inequality', Integers 25 (2025) A8 (zbMATH 8081397), with a corrigendum by Luca–Pomerance–Solé (Integers 25 (2025)): #{5040 < n ≤ x : σ(n) ≥ e^γ n log log n} ≪ x^ε. The content of the corrigendum is unchecked.; Nicolas, 'The sum of divisors function and the Riemann hypothesis', Ramanujan J. 58 (2022) 1113–1157 (zbMATH 7557859): effective RH-conditional bounds for σ(n)/n (Ramanujan's conditional expansion), the Robin-side analogue of ARITH-NICOLAS.; Ramanujan's prime inequality π(x)² < (ex/log x)π(x/e) (RH-adjacent). Unconditional for x ≥ exp(3361) (Johnston–Yang 2023, Thm 1.5) and for 38 358 837 683 ≤ x ≤ exp(103) (Johnston 2022, Thm 5.1); under RH for all x ≥ 38 358 837 683 (Dudek–Platt 2015, as cited by Johnston–Yang). Subham De, arXiv 2407.12052v7, claims x ≥ e^{490} using the announced 7/8 half-plane, so that claim is conditional and unrefereed.; RH-conditional Skewes upper bounds: Saouter–Trudgian–Demichel 2015 RH version (per zbMATH 6460495); C. Smith, York MSc thesis 2016, 1.397166161527·10^316 under RH (unpublished). Also an advertised Hughes–Smith–Platt 'new upper bound on Skewes number' (Imperial seminar, undated; no paper located).; Verified range of θ(x) < x: 2 < x ≤ 10^19 (Büthe 2018, eq. (1.7)), extending Platt–Trudgian 2016 (Math. Comp. 85, 1539–1547). It is a natural companion record to ARITH-SKEWES-LOWER.; Mishra–Sarkar, arXiv 2609.26787 (Sept 2026, unrefereed): a truncated Robin inequality equivalent to RH, proved for ω(n) ≤ 6, primorials, odd and squarefree n. Low priority; record only as unrefereed.

**Verifier critique.** Overall the frontier is accurate and well gated. These points need fixing.  (1) 'Independently proved twice' overstates the corroboration. Lamzouri wrote a second proof after AF appeared, and it leads to the same Montgomery-Taylor extremal problem. Its analytic input is BGST 2024 Lemma 5, and the proof of the underlying BGST Theorem 1 needed a correction (arXiv:2501.14545v3 §3, after Garunkštis-Paliulionytė; BGST say Lemma 5 survives). The Axiom Lean certificate for Lamzouri's Theorem 1.1 ASSUMES Lemma 5 and Riemann-von Mangoldt. AF prove their second moment themselves (Theorem 5.7), and their Lean statement is reported to be hypothesis-free. But the printed proof of Prop. 4.2 contains the misstatement Arias de Reyna flags. My reading puts it at the claim \|Re gamma - alpha_k\| >= D_rho for zeros of I' minus I. It is repairable. The first gate is the right one, and it should be stated as 'AF printed proof needs a local repair; Lamzouri's formal certificate is conditional on its analytic lemma'.  (2) The refereed kappa record should be credited jointly to Wu (Math. Z. 293 (2019), Theorem 1.2: kappa > .4172 and kappa* > .4074 for all Dirichlet L-functions, zeta included) and to PRZZ. PRZZ's §5.1 square-free argument is written out only for K = 2, while their numerics use K = 3.  (3) Bottleneck (a). The 'local-inequality refinements are capped at 0.6736-0.6738' applies only to Knausgård's second window. His own Corollary 7.9 gives the window-uniform cap 0.68185, which reuses AF's construction. So within bandwidth one there is still about 0.008 of theoretical headroom, and the AI-swarm values may keep creeping upward. The binding ceiling is AF/Knausgård's 0.6818, and it is not independently derived twice.  (4) Bottleneck (b) and negative result (5) overreach. Radziwill's I >= c/theta concerns Selberg-type mollification \|1 - zeta M\|^2, with c tied to the proportion of critical zeros. It does not bound the Levinson proportion. Feng's conditional 0.6107 at theta = 1 belongs to one mollifier family. CFKLTB (arXiv:2508.11108) show that optimising the derivative combination can matter more than the mollifier, and CIS's family optimum at theta = 1 is 0.5865. 'Mollifiers become competitive only with theta well beyond 1' is a heuristic, not a theorem. Negative result (6), Feng's remark (a), is conditional on the small-gap conjecture.  (5) Ingredient (i) misuses the Hua-Yang formula. 2 - 1/c*_lambda is derived for bandwidth lambda_bw <= 1 (it is defined with a min{1, ...}). Montgomery's F(alpha) changes form at \|alpha\| = 1 (under PCC, F = 1 beyond it), so extrapolating to lambda > 1 is meaningless. The formula also saturates near 0.889 at lambda of about 2.2 (my computation), not at 1. The valid evidence that wider support helps is AF §7.3 ('known on (-lambda0, lambda0) for all lambda0 gives 100%') and GLSS (PCC gives 100%).  (6) The next concrete step for kappa* is weakly motivated. AF §7.2(d) frames the sharp rank bound as a Chebyshev-Markov-Stieltjes problem using moments up to 2m. AF's own RH-conditional use of tr G^3 is for DISTINCT zeros with a cubic weight. The first conditional kappa* gain they cite needs the fourth moment (HL*(4), giving 13/18). Remark 6.1 also shows that reducing the bandwidth (lambda < 1) strictly lowers the second-moment constant ('No lambda < 1 improves the constants'). Before building anything, check whether a third moment, at reduced bandwidth, can raise the CMS lower bound on the rank at all. My expectation is that it may not. The RH-free CGG/BHB route would produce simple zeros N_s, not simple on-line zeros. Its unconditional comparator is therefore Knausgård's N_s >= 0.6780, and the obstruction is real: \|B zeta'(rho)\|^2 is a holomorphic function of rho only under RH.  (7) Historical arithmetic. '+0.061 from theta 1/2 to 4/7' compares Levinson's 0.3474 with 0.4088. Against the preceding theta = 1/2 record (Conrey 1983, 0.3658) the length gain is +0.043. The other increments (+0.0085 and +0.0062 after 1989; +0.265, or 65% relative) are correct.  (8) Negative results (1)-(4) and (7)-(8) are real and correctly stated, with one qualifier: AF label (d)-(f) as informal.

**Missing records flagged by verifier.** X. Wu, Math. Z. 293 (2019) 825-865 (arXiv:1802.09704), Theorem 1.2: kappa(chi) > .4172 and kappa*(chi) > .4074 for every Dirichlet character, zeta included. Refereed and independent of PRZZ, so the 5/12 record is shared (PRZZ Remark 1.1).; N. Robles, A. Roy, A. Zaharescu, J. Math. Anal. Appl. 434 (2016) 271-314: the crossed Conrey + Feng + BCY mollifier, which PRZZ credit with the 'technically difficult' combination.; Baluyot-Goldston-Suriajaya-Turnage-Butterbaugh, arXiv:2501.14545v3 §3: correction of the proof of the unconditional Montgomery theorem (Acta Arith. 214 (2024), Theorem 1), after Garunkštis-Paliulionytė. Lemmas 5 and 7 are stated unaffected. This is a load-bearing dependency for Lamzouri, Wang, Hua-Yang and others.; Lamzouri arXiv:2609.02882 Appendix A: the AxiomProver Lean certificate for Theorem 1.1 is conditional on BGST Lemma 5 and Riemann-von Mangoldt. Only Prop. 2.1 is certified unconditionally.; Conrey-Iwaniec-Soundararajan, Crelle 681 (2013): Remark giving kappa' = 0.5865 with optimal P (Dirichlet family), and Theorem 3 giving family-average simple on-line proportions >= 7/20 for GL2 twists and >= 1/200 for GL3 twists.; X. Wu, Q. J. Math. 67 (2016) 757-779: 60.261% simple zeros for the family of Dirichlet L-functions.; Alpöge-Furman Remark 7.1 (unrefereed, in Lean): unconditionally >= 85.838% (flat window) and 86.864% (quartic window) of zeros of xi' are simple and on the line, against Conrey's 79.874%; distinct >= 0.92919 / 0.93432. Wu 2015 §3 has 0.86957 for xi' zeros on the line, without simplicity.; Hua-Yang arXiv:2609.27808: growing-height prime-modulus family, 2 - 1/c*_lambda for bandwidth lambda_bw <= 1, giving C_MT when log T = o(log q).; B. Wang arXiv:2609.07918 (unrefereed): simple critical and distinct zeros in short intervals, by Lamzouri's method plus a short-interval BGST Montgomery theorem.; Angón-Ross-Tang-Zhao arXiv:2610.22927v2 (unrefereed): two, three and four consecutive moderate gaps (c <= 1/4) with densities >= 0.3450014, 0.1812517 and 0.0175021, by AF/Lamzouri methods.; Y. Lamzouri arXiv:2611.14486 (10 Nov 2026, unrefereed): small gaps, lambda0 = 0.7892 < 4/5, by unconditional pair correlation (versus Zhao's 1 - 2.36e-6).; F. Aryan arXiv:2610.12853 (Oct 2026): odd (imaginary-part) companion of the Fejér pair-correlation identity, with an exposition of the matrix argument. Possible extra input on off-line clustering.; Knausgård arXiv:2609.33043 is at v2 (7 Oct 2026). The ledger should cite the version.; Ki-Lee, Funct. Approx. 47 (2012) 79-87, Theorem 5 (>70% distinct): still unverified. The Crossref abstract does not mention distinct zeros, so the record should stay flagged as disputed.


## DBN: DBN: the de Bruijn–Newman constant Λ (classical ζ and generalized L-functions), with zero dynamics under the backward heat flow

| LED-DBN-1 | Existence and range of the de Bruijn–Newman constant Λ (classical ζ) | −∞ < Λ ≤ 1/2: Normalization used throughout this ledger (Polymath/Rodgers–Tao): H_t(z) = ∫_0^∞ e^{tu²} Φ(u) cos(zu) du, Φ(u) = Σ_{n≥1} (2π²n⁴e^{9u} − 3πn²e^{5u}) exp(−πn²e^{4u}), H_0(z) = (1/8) ξ(1/2 + iz/2). H_t solves the backward heat equation ∂_t H_t = −∂_zz H_t. Pólya: if H_t has only real zeros, so does H_{t'} for every t' > t. de Bruijn (1950): H_t has only real zeros for t ≥ 1/2. Newman (1976): there is an absolute constant Λ with −∞ < Λ ≤ 1/2 such that H_t has only real zeros if and only if t ≥ Λ. RH | unconditional_theorem | C. M. Newman; N. | LED-DBN-2 | de Bruijn strip contraction (how an initial zero strip turns into an upper bound for Λ) | Λ ≤ Δ²/2 for an initial zero strip of half-width Δ: Let φ be even and integrable with φ(u) = O(e^{−\|u\|^b}) for some b > 2, G(z) = ∫ φ(u) e^{izu} du, and G_t(z) = ∫ e^{tu²} φ(u) e^{izu} du. If every root of G lies in \|Im z\| ≤ Δ, then for t > 0 every root of G_t lies in \|Im z\| ≤ max(Δ² − 2t, 0)^{1/2}. Hence Λ ≤ Δ²/2. The classical strip 0 < Re ρ < 1 gives Δ = 1 and so Λ ≤ 1/2. A zero-free half-plane Re s > σ, combined with the functional equation, gives Δ = 2σ − 1 and so Λ ≤ (2σ−1)²/2. For σ = 7/8 this is Λ ≤ 9/32 = 0.28125 (Hakimoglu-Brown § | unconditional_theorem | N. | LED-DBN-4 | Zero dynamics of H_t under the backward heat flow (Csordas–Smith–Varga) | ∂_t x_k = 2 Σ' 1/(x_k − x_j); CSV lower bound Λ > −4.379×10⁻⁶: For Λ < t (in particular Λ < t ≤ 0 if Λ < 0), the zeros of H_t are real, simple and avoid 0 (CSV Cor. 1). The positive zeros satisfy ∂_t x_k = 2 Σ_{j≠k} 1/(x_k − x_j) (CSV Lemma 2.4). This is a repulsion: zeros spread apart as t increases. A negative Λ would therefore forbid sufficiently close adjacent zeros ('Lehmer pairs') at t = 0, and the CSV criterion (Thm 1) turns a high-quality Lehmer pair into a lower bound for Λ. **Verifier critique.** best_established is correct as stated: 0 ≤ Λ ≤ 0.2 is peer-reviewed. MathWorld (last updated 16 Nov 2026) and Planat–Raymond's Table 1 both list Platt–Trudgian 2021 as the last published bound. Two provenance caveats belong next to it. (a) Λ ≤ 0.2 is a one-paragraph corollary that relies on Polymath Table 1 row 2. Polymath §10 documents that row's canopy only by a lower bound at N = N0 and an 'expect to be able to verify' statement; only the barrier runs are reported as complete. §10 also swaps the (ii)/(iii) labels. (b) Rodgers–Tao's journal version of record contains the §8 energy-boundary treatment that arXiv v5 corrects, and no erratum has been issued. Dobner's independent proof covers this. The unrefereed cluster list is incomplete. It omits: - Planat arXiv:2609.37164v2 Thm 7.2, Λ < 0.157371218 (unconditional in form); - the same paper's Thm 8.3, Λ < 0.149345, relative to Raymond's interfaces (CA-type); - the same paper's conditional checkpoints 0.1718 and 0.16836; - Raymond, Zenodo 22626161, Λ < 0.163297 (U-form); - Raymond, Zenodo 23272725, 0.116398 under H7/8. It appears in DBN-14 only as Planat's quotation. Historical improvement: - '0.2 came only from extending RH verification' should say it came from the new RH height combined with the already-computed Polymath §10 row-2 barrier and canopy. - The lower-bound list should note that Stopple's −1.12929×10⁻⁷ is in the λ = Λ/4 normalization (≈ −4.52×10⁻⁷ in H_t). Bottleneck: the 1/log T heuristic is quoted accurately as a heuristic, and the C ≈ 5–6 → 4.4 arithmetic checks. - The claim that every quantitative bound goes through a Polymath-type (i)/(ii)/(iii) criterion is not accurate for the 2026 cluster. Hakimoglu-Brown, Raymond's translated probes, Planat's classical-strip comparison and Gordon replace the canopy (ii) with a global strip-contraction comparison driven by collective zero repulsion and logarithmic derivatives. Planat–Raymond replace it with collision exclusion plus remote reality. The finite RH height (i) and a barrier remain. - Hakimoglu-Brown's 0.13963 uses only the old 3.06×10¹⁰ height, so the 7/8 input is exactly the 'massive expansion of zero-free regions' escape clause that Polymath names. - 'Enters linearly through de Bruijn's Δ²/2' is wrong: the initial strip enters quadratically, as Δ²/2 = (2σ−1)²/2. Negative results: - Item (3), Rodgers–Tao pp.3–4, is a remark ('do not appear to be sufficient'), not a theorem. - The ACM 'linear combinations' point is, in the body of the paper, the observation that Pólya's setup cannot be generalized without a proper functional equation. It is not a theorem about Lehmer pairs. - Item (6) should say that failure of PF_∞ was already forced by Schoenberg's characterization; only the specific order 5 is new, and PF order is not the RH-relevant positivity notion. - Items (1), (2), (4), (5) and (7) are correctly stated. For (5), cite Lagarias–Montague Thm 2.1(2). Breakthrough list and next step: these are sensible. The proposed explicit version of Polymath Thm 1.5(i) is well targeted, because the ineffective C is exactly why Planat–Raymond need a separate Euler-product barrier at infinity. The replay proposal correctly respects X ≤ 2·T_PT. The y0 = O(1/√log x) shape is the drafter's own conjecture and should be labelled as such. Overall the classification of the 2026 claims (U/CA/C7/8, all unrefereed and none replayed) is sound and conservative. The ledger correctly avoids promoting any of them.

Gordon-hybrid density–jet continuation with 4803 rows; unconditional in form; unrefereed; 0.000285 below Gordon's endpoint.; Planat, arXiv:2609.37164v2 Thm 8.3: Λ < 0.149345 (74672416998643/5×10¹⁴), a head-pair extension of Raymond's translated probes after re-running Raymond's frozen replay (0.149395073827688). Assumes Raymond's uniform true-function approximation and source/error inequalities (CA/audit-relative); unrefereed.; Planat, arXiv:2609.37164v2 §6: conditional checkpoints Λ < 0.1718 and Λ < 0.16836 from a hybrid of Kornaus's collision exclusion. The author presents them as conditional on certificate-validity hypotheses.; Raymond, Zenodo 10.5281/zenodo.22626161 (8 Sep 2026): Λ ≤ 287/2000 + ½((287/2000) log 4)² < 0.163297 via the Polymath heat-flow criterion with phase-correlated certificates. U-form, unrefereed.; Raymond, Zenodo 10.5281/zenodo.23272725 (10 Oct 2026): Λ ≤ 0.116397726408291 < 0.116398, conditional on ζ ≠ 0 for Re s > 7/8. Superseded by Raymond 2026b's claim of 0.107965.; Ki–Kim–Lee 2009 (Adv. Math. 222): the de Bruijn–Newman constants Λ^(m) of the derivatives Ξ_λ^(m) are non-increasing in m with lim_{m→∞} Λ^(m) ≤ 0, unconditionally (zbMATH 1175.30023; restated in Lagarias–Montague p.3).; Mosaic Intelligence, Zenodo 21175533: certified intermediate rows Λ ≤ 0.197 and Λ ≤ 0.197262405 at the Polymath/Platt–Trudgian site, a certified version of the 2020 Dwars blog value. Unrefereed and AI-generated.; Platt's GRH verification for primitive Dirichlet characters of modulus ≤ 4×10⁵ up to h_m = max(10⁸/m, 200 + A(m)10⁷/m), Math. Comp. 2016: the finite-verification input for DBN-17.; Stopple, 'Lehmer pairs revisited', Exp. Math. 26 (2017) 45–53 (arXiv:1508.05870), refining the Lehmer-pair criterion under RH (cited as RT ref [26]); and Simonič, arXiv:1612.08627, which relates Lehmer pairs to derivatives of Hardy's Z.; Newman–Wu, Bull. AMS 57 (2020) 595–614 (arXiv:1901.06596): survey of de Bruijn–Newman-type constants in statistical physics (Lee–Yang measures). It is the reference for the non-number-theoretic analogues the ledger alludes to.

**Verifier critique.** Overall, the frontier is well sourced, and most numbers survive full-text checking. Errors and gaps found:  (1) STATUS ERROR, frontier (iv): Bellotti-Wong is not unrefereed. The arXiv comment reads 'Accepted by Math. Comp.', and Crossref gives Mathematics of Computation, DOI 10.1090/mcom/4133, online 14 Nov 2025. The refereed leading-coefficient record for S(T) and N(T) is therefore 0.10076. Even so, 'record' depends on height. As stated, HSW Cor. 1.4's second branch (0.1095 log T + 0.2042 log log T + 3.0305) is numerically smaller than BW Cor. 1.5 for e^{2.85} < T < e^{506.85}. BW's closed form wins only above about 10^{220}.  (2) NEGATIVE RESULT (d) IS WRONG. Rankin's exponent-pair optimum (about 0.164511) was beaten long before Bombieri-Iwaniec, by multi-dimensional exponential-sum methods: Titchmarsh 1942 19/116 = 0.16379, Min 1949 15/92, Haneke 1962 6/37, Kolesnik 173/1067, 35/216, 139/858. So 'all progress below about 0.1645 came from Bombieri-Iwaniec-type input' is false. The historical_improvement phrase 'classical exponent-pair refinements (1924-1985)' is mislabelled for the same reason.  (3) GROWTH-19 carries a false generalisation into the frontier: zeta is not the only object with a sub-Weyl bound. Dirichlet L-functions in the t-aspect have one, and so do prime-power moduli in the q-aspect (Milićević 2016). The accurate statement is 'none beyond GL(1)', which is HMSS's own abstract wording.  (4) Bottleneck (1): I re-derived the drafter's bookkeeping from Bourgain §3-§4 and it is correct. \|S\|^6 << M^3 T^{1/2+3theta/2} gives 1/12+theta/4 (13/84 at theta=2/7). (4.1) and (4.2) cover the middle range iff mu' >= 43/279. The Bourgain-range endpoint is (128mu'-4)/39 = 0.40537 - 3.28 delta. However, the claim that larger improvements need new estimates near alpha ≈ 0.387-0.393 is relative to Bourgain's two §4 inputs only. It has not been tested against ANTEDB's full table of beta(alpha) and exponent pairs, so it should stay labelled as a conjecture of the bookkeeping. Bourgain also says he used the Huxley-Watt treatment of the second spacing problem for the main range and invoked Huxley's more advanced [H1] only to extend the M-range. 'Second spacing is the binding obstruction' is a fair reading, not a stated theorem.  (5) Negative result (g) overstates. ABR I and II match only the upper tail, up to constants (C y e^{-2y} above; c y e^{-2y} e^{-y^2/log log T} below). The lower-tail bound c^{-1} y^{-c} is not sharp. Both ABR papers are arXiv-only per zbMATH and Crossref, so frontier (vi) rests on unrefereed preprints. GROWTH-12 is labelled unconditional_theorem while GROWTH-13 is labelled unrefereed, which is inconsistent.  (6) Negative result (f) cites the wrong theorem: the maximal Gál sum is dlBT Théorème 1.1 (1·4), not 1.2. The inference that sqrt2 is hard to beat with GCD resonators should mention the Bondarenko-Darbar-Hagen-Heap-Seip dichotomy (Bull. LMS 55 (2023) 2963-2975, read at abstract level). It says either the zeta maximum lower bound improves or Dirichlet L-functions take Bondarenko-Seip-size values, so the resonance and Gál-sum linkage is not the only route.  (7) Frontier (v) gives only the RH Omega for S(t). The unconditional record, Tsang 1986 Omega_±((log t/log log t)^{1/3}), is still the best per Dobner 2021 (arXiv 2101.01747) and is missing as an entry.  (8) Negative results (a), (b), (c) and (e) are real and correctly stated. Verified: the Kusmin/Landau sharpness, the 0.507/0.5075 and 0.541 barriers, Bourgain's 'essentially optimal A_6', CCM's 'unattainable ... without a significant breakthrough in estimating certain prime number sums', and CS's 'limit of existing methods' framing. Bottleneck (3) is accurate. Bottleneck (5), about FHK convergence in law, is a plausible interpretation and is not sourced.  (9) Minor: the Ramachandra-Sankaranarayanan year is 1993, not ~2005. Simonič is J. Number Theory 231 (2022). I checked that Simonič's Corollary 1 (0.96 for S(t)) survives replacing the flawed PT15 input by HSW Cor. 1.4 plus Platt's computation. Revers's 0.611 margin issue is reproduced: -5.0e-7 at t=4.96e7 with the printed coefficients, +1.3e-6 under favourable rounding, so the claim stays undecided pending an interval replay.  (10) No improvement of mu(1/2) <= 13/84 was found (arXiv API searches, harvested metadata, web search, ANTEDB live table). HMSS's withdrawal (9 Oct 2026, 'withdrawn by Prahlad Sharma') is confirmed on the arXiv abs page.

**Missing records flagged by verifier.** Unconditional Omega for S(t): Tsang 1986 (Acta Arith. 46), S(t) = Omega_±((log t/log log t)^{1/3}), still the best unconditional result per Dobner 2021 (arXiv 2101.01747). Dobner also proves unconditional Gaussian-shape lower bounds for meas{S(t) >= V} for sqrt(log log T) <= V << (log T/log log T)^{1/3}.; Sub-Weyl in the depth/q-aspect: Milićević, 'Sub-Weyl subconvexity for Dirichlet L-functions to prime power moduli', Compositio Math. 152 (2016) 825-875. This is needed to correct GROWTH-19 and GROWTH-20.; Bondarenko-Darbar-Hagen-Heap-Seip, 'A dichotomy for extreme values of zeta and Dirichlet L-functions', Bull. LMS 55 (2023) 2963-2975 (arXiv 2302.08285): large Dedekind-zeta values on the critical line imply either a better max-\|zeta\| lower bound or Bondarenko-Seip-size Dirichlet L-values.; Arguin-Bailey, 'Lower bounds for the large deviations of Selberg's CLT', Mathematika 71 (2024), DOI 10.1112/mtk.70002 (arXiv 2403.19803): unconditional lower bounds at sigma = 1/2 + delta/log T, recovering sharp lower moment bounds. Also Arguin-Bailey, IMRN 2023, 20574-20612 (unconditional upper bound for 0 < alpha < 2) as a primary entry.; Carneiro-Chandee 2011 (J. Number Theory 131, 363-384) and Carneiro-Chirre-Milinovich 2019 (Publ. Mat. 63, 601-661): RH bounds with the improved o(1) = O(1/log log t) for (log 2)/2 and 1/4, plus CCM19's bounds for S_n(t).; Platt 2017 (Math. Comp. 86, 'Isolating some non-trivial zeros of zeta') and the LMFDB zero database as the primary source for \|S(T)\| <= 2.5167 on T <= 3.061e10, together with Platt-Trudgian 2021 (Bull. LMS 53) RH to 3e12 as a context record.; Explicit unconditional bound for S_1(t): Trudgian 2016 \|S_1(t)\| <= 0.059 log t + 3.054 for t >= 1, as quoted by Simonič. Check whether it is affected by the Trudgian erratum chain.; First-order short-interval maxima as primary records: Najnudel (RH; Probab. Theory Related Fields 2018) and Arguin-Belius-Bourgade-Radziwiłł-Soundararajan (unconditional; Comm. Pure Appl. Math. 2019, DOI 10.1002/cpa.21791).; Rate of convergence in Selberg's CLT: Tsang 1984 (unconditional) and Roberts (arXiv 2308.09679; RH rate sqrt(log_4 T)/sqrt(log log T) in Dudley distance).; Chirre-Mahatab, 'Large values of the argument of the Riemann zeta-function and its iterates' (arXiv 2006.04288, J. Number Theory 2021): Omega results for S_n(t) and S_n(sigma,t), the direct predecessor of Zhao 2026.; Inoue-Kobayashi-Toma (arXiv 2510.14309): explicit extreme values of S(t+h)-S(t) in short intervals; constant still to be extracted.; Montgomery 1977 (Comment. Math. Helv. 52): RH Omega result for log\|zeta(1/2+it)\| with explicit constant (1/20, unverified) and for S(t), as a primary entry rather than history-only.; Off-line context used downstream by GROWTH-08: Hiary-Leong-Yang explicit 1-line bounds (Funct. Approx. 73 (2025) 53-89) and Yang's explicit critical-strip bounds \|zeta(sigma_k+it)\| <= 1.546 t^{1/(2^k-2)} log t (arXiv 2301.03165).

**Verifier critique.** Overall the frontier is broadly accurate. The main bottleneck it names is right: the two largest 2026 advances (Siegel-zero exclusion and QRH) are unrefereed and AI-originated. Several specific statements need fixing.  (1) Bottleneck (b) misattributes. The Observatory's own secondary note (history/recent_developments_2024_2026.md Sec 7) says experts 'do not expect the metaplectic method to reach 1/2', not that it cannot go 'below about 7/8'. The follow-ups already claim slightly below 7/8: 0.874957 (Liu), 0.8748867 (Xu-Sang), 0.874887 for all number fields (Liu), 0.8744683 (Liu, Hexagon) and 0.874462 (Chang-Wang 2611.00924), all conditional on the 7/8 inputs. Liu proves optimality only 'within the specified exponent comparison system'. The Observatory's DOSSIER.md finds a structural floor B > 2/3 for the paper2 system. Restate (b) as: refinements stall near 0.8745 inside the current comparison systems, there is an internal floor of 2/3 for the 11/12 system, and nobody expects 1/2.  (2) Negative result 1 is wrong in a load-bearing way. Davenport-Heilbronn and Epstein functions have N(sigma1,sigma2,T) ≍ T off-line zeros out of ≍ T log T zeros in total. That is infinitely many, but a proportion that tends to zero, not 'a positive density' and not 'asymptotic to T'. The moral (the Euler product is essential) survives.  (3) best_established omits that Lu-Zaman-Zhao is now refereed (Math. Comp., online 16 Oct 2026), and that Rezvyakova 2024 is published (Izv. Math. 90, 2026). Khale's explicit VK region is also refereed (Q. J. Math. 2024).  (4) historical_improvement: 'zero-free region shape unchanged since 1918' should be limited to the q-aspect. In the t-aspect the Vinogradov-Korobov shape holds for L(s,chi), now explicit (Khale 2024). The date also conflicts with LFUN-03's '1899'. The exceptional-zero check history should add Platt-Trudgian 2610.29018: sigma > 0 for real chi with q <= 10^9, and odd q <= 10^10. 'Linnik: 0.5 in 34 years' is misleading; the drop 5.5 -> 5 happened 1992-2011, with nothing since.  (5) Bottleneck (c) and the Linnik items. The Heath-Brown remark is confirmed only as reported by Xylouris: L = 2.4+eps if the zero-free region holds with arbitrarily large c1 and there is no exceptional zero. So 'a half-plane gives L <= 12/5+eps' is a sound reading, and with a non-log-free density (qT)^{A(1-sigma)+eps} for sigma <= theta one gets L = A+eps by a routine argument. No QRH paper states any Linnik consequence (grep of the 7/8 and 11/12 texts and the follow-ups found none), so the draft is right that the lemma is unwritten. The claim that 'the worst case is not the exceptional-zero regime' is Observatory reasoning and is not sourced. The open question 'anything better than 2+eps without GRH-type input?' is mis-posed: GRH itself gives only 2+eps, and Bruna 2603.25612 gets 2+eps under GLH. The open question is whether anything breaks 2 at all.  (6) Constants. The Siegel exclusion constant is 'c <= 1/64' (Guo Remark 1.2), not '<= 10^-2'. 'The effective class-number bound is only h(-d) >> log\|d\|' drops the Euler factor and applies only to imaginary quadratic fields.  (7) Negative result 4's claim that the Siegel-zero world is 'consistent with all known sieve tools' is interpretation, not a cited theorem. The input description (interpolation determinant / pigeonhole plus Masser zero estimate) is confirmed by Kerr and Banks.  (8) possible_external_ingredients cites 'Kominers; Frei-Kai' without identifiers. These could not be located and should be sourced or removed.  (9) The QRH status is understated in one respect. OpenAI's repository claims a Lean formalization of the 7/8 theorem (lean/docs/003.md; Kimura independently notes it), and a grep of the DirichletL Lean tree finds no 'sorry'. The 'set aside' remark about the 7/8 paper (Quanta, secondary) should therefore be paired with the claimed formalization, and compiling it should rank alongside the SiegelZeros comparator in next_concrete_step.

**Missing records flagged by verifier.** Platt-Trudgian, 'Zeros of quadratic Dirichlet L-functions', arXiv 2610.29018v1 (21 Oct 2026, unrefereed): L(sigma,chi) != 0 for all sigma > 0 for real primitive chi with q <= 10^9, and odd chi with q <= 10^10 (rigorous ball arithmetic, lfunctions package). It supersedes Watkins 2004 (3e8, odd) in the real-zero verification history.; Best claimed QRH exponent: theta = 0.874462057... (Chang-Wang arXiv 2611.00924v1, 31 Oct 2026). It is conditional on the cited inputs and built on Liu's B* = 0.8744683 (Hexagon 2610.00655v1). Earlier steps: Liu 2610.12234 (0.874957, Lean claimed), Xu-Sang 2610.15291 (0.8748867), Liu 2610.26835 (0.874887 for Hecke L over every number field).; Cureton-Thorner (Axiom Math / AxiomProver), Hexagon 2610.00200v2: zero-free Re s > 31/32 for every Dedekind zeta function and finite-order Hecke L over every number field, with Lean formalization (cited by Liu 2610.26835 and Kimura 2610.21511). Unrefereed. It deserves its own record rather than a mention inside LFUN-03.; Ji, arXiv 2611.16665v2: zeta_{Q(i)} has no zeros in Re s > 599/600 via quartic Gauss sums. An independent-variant QRH method; unrefereed.; Guo arXiv 2610.30476 Remark 1.2: Siegel-zero gap with c = 1/64 for all q >= 3 (Appendix A). Kimura 2610.21511 Thm 1: 0.005965 for log q >= 35.0131. These are the current explicit constants.; Bruna, arXiv 2603.25612 (Mar 2026): under the generalized Lindelof hypothesis, the least prime p = a mod q satisfies p <<_eps q^{2+eps}. Conditional Linnik record.; Explicit L(1,chi) lower bounds: Bennett-Martin-O'Bryant-Rechnitzer (2018), L(1,chi) > 12/sqrt q for primitive quadratic chi with q > 6677; Mossinghoff-Starichkova-Trudgian (2022), \|L(1,psi)\| >= 1/(9.7 log q) for primitive non-quadratic psi (both as cited in LZZ 2602.03626 p.3).; Restricted-range explicit zero-free regions for Dirichlet L: Ahn-Kwon (2014) for small \|t\| and Louboutin (2015) for t = 0 (cited in LZZ p.1). Bordignon (2019/2020) and Morrill-Trudgian explicit real-zero bounds 1 - c/(sqrt q log^2 q) as an all-q analytic record.; Mosunov-Jacobson (2016): unconditional class groups of all imaginary quadratic fields with \|d\| < 2^40. Garcia-Grenie-Lee-Molteni (2026): h <= 2000 under GRH, now unconditional by Kimura Thm 2. These are the inputs and comparators for LFUN-07.; Bondarenko-Heap arXiv 2608.07399 / 2610.26038: on RH, an infinite family of Siegel zeros forces liminf of normalized zeta-zero gaps < 0.4733; also half-sized gaps on RH. A conditional link between Siegel zeros and zeta-zero spacing.; Thorner-Zhao arXiv 2601.04189: standard zero-free regions with no Landau-Siegel zeros for new Rankin-Selberg and triple-product families. Cited only as a next step; it merits a record for the GL(n) Siegel-zero sub-frontier.; de Faveri arXiv 2610.04045: optimal large sieve for fixed-order (n >= 3) Hecke characters. A candidate input to cubic/sextic-character methods behind QRH.

**Verifier critique.** Overall the frontier is accurate and well-sourced, and the current-best claims all survived full-text checks. Corrections and sharpening:  (1) Sixth-moment lower-constant claims overstate what extending the twisted fourth moment would do. The bottleneck item, breakthrough (B), next step 2 and MOM-10 caveat (2) all say this. Durkan–Page's Theorem 1 is the quotient (∫\|ζ\|^4\|B\|^2)^2/∫\|ζ\|^2\|B\|^4 with B=A+χĀ, and the denominator needs a χ-twisted second moment with the fourth power of the amplifier. That is admissible only for θ_k<1/(2k), i.e. length T^{1/4} for k=2. Both constraints sit at 1/4. Pushing BBLR beyond ϑ=1/4 alone would therefore not 'immediately' raise 34.4 c_3. One also needs the denominator past T^{1/4}: BCR's 17/33 could in principle allow A^2 up to T^{17/33}, i.e. A up to T^{17/66}, for the untwisted ν=0 piece, but the χ-twisted pieces I_{2,ν} need separate treatment.  (2) The upper-bound bottleneck phrasing is imprecise. The RS/HRS principle needs a higher twisted moment with a sharp-order bound (T(log T)^{k'^2}, k'>k), not merely 'T^{1+o(1)}'. A T^{1+o(1)} twisted sixth moment would not give sharp log-powers. M_6 ≪ T^{1+ε} is better described as a weak (sixth-power-mean) form of Lindelöf; it gives only ζ ≪ t^{1/6+ε} pointwise. The real two-part obstruction is: (a) no twisted moment above the fourth; (b) without RH there is no Soundararajan-type pointwise upper bound for log\|ζ\|, so the twisted fourth moment cannot be leveraged beyond k=2 as Radziwiłł did on RH (k<2.18).  (3) Constants. Harper §6 locates the doubly exponential loss exactly: a Gaussian 'random Euler product' heuristic with polynomial length T^{2/k} recovers e^{−k^2 log k+O(k^2)}. Making that rigorous requires mean values of Dirichlet polynomials of length T^{2/k} raised to the k-th power (beyond the mean-value-theorem range). The open question 'is the doubly exponential loss intrinsic?' should cite this. It is a concrete length barrier, not an unexplained loss.  (4) The historical-improvement line 'sixth-moment lower constant: 2c_3-type bounds → 34.4 c_3' is unsupported. Soundararajan's 2c_k refers to k ≥ 7. The prior sixth-moment constants (Conrey–Ghosh 1992; Soundararajan 1995; Page, JNT 280 (2026)) were not extracted. Page's JNT paper is real (Crossref DOI 10.1016/j.jnt.2025.08.017) but not on arXiv, so its sixth- and eighth-moment constants remain unverified.  (5) The negative results cited are real and correctly stated: BCR p.2–3 and Appendix, BBLR pp.4–5, dFP §1.2, Harper §6, the Lindelöf equivalence (dFP p.1), Radziwiłł 1207.6583 (abstract), the Bourgain–Watt 2017 withdrawal (verbatim) and DRZ v2 (verbatim). Pseudomoments item (6) is abstract-level and vague. Gerspach's 'three ranges' concern pseudomoments of ζ^α with α ≥ 1 and q ≤ 1/2; the ledger should not state a precise deviation without reading BBSSZ or Gerspach.  (6) Normalization: the area's M_k warning is right. Also, Florea's survey writes I_6 for the twelfth moment, which is a third convention.  (7) de Faveri–Pandey dependency: Theorem 1.1 (37/30) rests only on Theorem 1.3 and the classical bounds. The δ_k for k≠6 and the twelfth-moment saving additionally use Trudgian–Yang exponent pairs and Bourgain 13/84, so the audit targets differ. Next step 1's target 'M_6 ≪ T^{6/5}' should be 'T^{6/5−δ}' (Iwaniec's threshold is strict).  (8) Next step 3 (certified sixth-moment computation to 10^7) is honest about non-rigour. Even certified, it is a finite check far below the heights already explored non-rigorously by Hiary–Odlyzko (near the 10^23rd zero). Its value is rigour, not evidence about asymptotics.  (9) Status updates: Ng–Shen–Wong (eighth moment) is now JEMS 28 (2026) 4175–4237; Curran 'Correlations' is Mathematika 70(4) (2024). Harper 2013, Curran–Heycock 2024 and Tingyu Tao 2024 remain preprints (zbMATH). 'Tao (2024)' must be identified as Tingyu Tao.

**Missing records flagged by verifier.** Short-interval mean square: Bourgain–Watt (IMRN 2018) Theorem 3, I(t, t^{1273/4053+ε}) = O(log t). This is the surviving record; the withdrawn 1709.04340 claim of 7159/22824 targeted exactly this.; Mean square and Ω-results for E(T): Heath-Brown (1978), ∫E(t)^2 dt = c T^{3/2} + O(T^{5/4} log^2 T), with later error improvements (Meurman, Preissmann, Lau–Tsang; from memory). Good (1977), E(T) = Ω(T^{1/4}), confirmed via Simonič–Starichkova p.1. Hafner–Ivić Ω± refinements.; Motohashi's explicit spectral formula for the fourth moment (Acta Math. 170 (1993) 181–220, Crossref), and E_2(T) = Ω(T^{1/2}). This is the structural basis of MOM-02 and of the 'Hecke series H_j(1/2)' bottleneck.; Iwaniec (1980) short-interval fourth moment ∫_T^{T+T^{2/3}} \|ζ\|^4 ≪ T^{2/3+ε} (memory; dFP §1.1 cites Iwa80 for its use). This is the engine behind M_12 ≪ T^{2+ε} and E_2 ≪ T^{2/3+ε}.; Power moments above the twelfth: bounds ∫\|ζ\|^A ≪ T^{…} for A > 12 using μ(1/2) ≤ 13/84 (Bourgain). See Durkan–Palojärvi–Trudgian Remark 2 (T^{k/6+ε} for k > 12 and a stronger form for k ≥ 178/13) and Ivić Ch. 8.; Page, 'Unconditional lower bounds for the sixth and eighth moments of the Riemann zeta function', J. Number Theory 280 (2026) 318–369 (DOI 10.1016/j.jnt.2025.08.017). Its sixth- and eighth-moment constants are not recorded and are needed to decide whether 410 c_4 is the current unconditional eighth-moment record.; Durkan–Page Theorems 3–9 (arXiv 2606.27323): unconditional lower constants for ∫\|ζ″\|^6 (0.0231 c_3) and for joint moments of ζ, ζ′ and ζ″ versus the Keating–Wei conjectures, e.g. ∫\|ζ\|^2\|ζ′\|^4 ≥ 2.216 c_3 vs 2.783 c_3.; Mollifier-type twisted second moment length θ < 4/7 (Conrey 1989), still the record for structured coefficients, with two-piece mollifier extensions (Bui–Conrey–Young 2011; Pratt–Robles–Zaharescu–Zeindler, arXiv 1802.10521). Relevant to MOM-14's 'structured coefficients' line.; Kovaleva (arXiv 2401.13725): an asymptotic for the shifted fourth moment with shifts up to T^{3/2−ε}, an approximate spectral expansion of the error, and the (2,2) moment of moments, which partially refutes a Bailey–Keating conjecture. Cited in MOM-17 only through Curran.; Bui–Hall–Subira Jorge (arXiv 2511.14415): an asymptotic for the fourth moment times the fourth power of a d_r-type amplifier of length T^{1/8−δ}, uniform in r. This is a distinct twisted-moment record and should be its own row rather than a footnote.; Conditional sharp bounds for short-interval moments ∫_T^{T+T^θ}\|ζ\|^{2k} (Harper §1 remark; Ivić). Also discrete moments at zeros (Gonek; Milinovich–Ng; Kirila; Heap–Li–Zhao negative discrete moments, ANT 16 (2022)). Adjacent to scope, but often confused with I_k.; Explicit Atkinson formula and explicit E(T) bounds: Simonič–Starichkova (arXiv 2105.06821) give an explicit O(log^2 T) remainder and an explicit E(T) ≪ T^{1/3+ε}; Dona–Zuniga Alterman (arXiv 2104.14253). These are currently only in MOM-01's history.

**Verifier critique.** 1. best_established and historical_improvement are outdated on the Weil-positivity window. The claim 'not extended since Yoshida 1992 (λ^2 = 2)' / 'no rigorous proof for λ^2 > 2' misses X. Zhu, arXiv 2608.24827v2 (Sep 2026, unrefereed). Zhu claims Q(f) >= 8.9e-18 \|\|f\|\|^2 for supp f ⊆ [-0.8, 0.8] (λ = e^{0.8}, λ^2 ≈ 4.95, with prime powers 2, 3, 4 inside). He also claims a two-sided enclosure 8.9e-18 <= λ*(0.8) <= 2.27e-17 and a simple, even window ground state with certified sector separations (Theorem 6.2), i.e. CCM 'missing step (1)' at one fixed scale. Caveats: single author; an earlier draft's support-2.38 certificate was retracted; the lower-bound matrix is assembled in non-interval 50-digit mpmath with a priori quadrature bounds and a floating-point Cholesky residual. By the project's own rule, this is 'high precision plus error budget', not a fully directed-rounding certificate. ACL 2611.13129 Section 8.7 also notes that S = {∞, 2} with 2 < λ^2 < 2.25 is still in the Connes–Consani regime.  2. The proposed 'meaningful_incremental_breakthrough' is partly already claimed and partly mis-scoped. (a) Positivity plus a simple, even ground state at λ^2 = 3 or 5 is essentially what Zhu claims at λ^2 ≈ 4.95. Since ε_λ is non-increasing in λ, positivity at λ^2 ≈ 4.95 implies it at λ^2 = 3. The real incremental step is therefore an independent ball-arithmetic re-verification of Zhu's certificate (Arb/FLINT enclosure of the digamma, spherical Bessel and A_L assembly), followed by extension to larger L. (b) The analytic variant, 'a two-sided Fuchs-ladder estimate ... for all λ in some unbounded range', is NOT incremental. ACL Proposition 8.17 and Suzuki Corollary 1.2 make ε_λ non-increasing, so ε_λ > 0 on any unbounded λ-set implies ε_λ >= 0 for all λ, which is RH (ACL Remark 3.5 says the lower-bound direction implies QW_λ >= 0). The ledger's 'not RH-equivalent at any fixed λ' is true but irrelevant for an unbounded range. Only bounded-range lower bounds are incremental.  3. The bottleneck is accurately identified as RH-equivalent positivity in several guises, and the three CCM/ACL gaps are correctly stated. But 'Galerkin truncations give upper sections only and can never certify' needs qualifying. Naive Rayleigh–Ritz truncation is upper-only, but finite reductions whose discards have a definite sign DO give lower certificates at fixed windows: Yoshida's finite calculation, Zhu's one-stroke frequency reduction (Theorem 1.1), and Groskin's cutoff rule (arXiv 2607.02828), where a negative eigenvalue in [-B_T, 0) certifies nothing. The real obstruction is uniformity in λ. Zhu's Theorem 1.4 shows that any pointwise-envelope certificate must resolve frequencies up to T_1(L) = 2π e^{A_L}, where A_L ~ 4e^L, so the cost is doubly exponential in L. Meanwhile the margin collapses at the Landau–Widom rate. This barrier belongs in both the bottleneck and negative_results.  4. The negative results I checked are real and correctly stated: Conrey–Li (constants exact; the H(E) witness is uncertified floating point, and only the F(W) failure is computation-free via Sarnak); Bellissard (n+ = 1, n- = 0); Endres–Steiner (abstract); the Lax–Phillips quote; ACL Remark 7.4; Suzuki's Corollary 1.6 remark; Yakaboylu v17. Corrections: (a) The 'Groskin negative eigenvalues disputed' item is not a dispute: Groskin v4 itself attributes them to the cutoff T = 800, and de Andrade Silva agrees. (b) Kim et al. 2607.24830 is not a negative result about Suzuki's D_{a,θ}; it concerns A_a. (c) Sierra's fine-tuning condition has -θ(E). (d) Missing negative results: Bombieri–Garrett, arXiv 2002.07929, Corollary 69: under RH plus Montgomery's pair-correlation CONJECTURE, at most 94% of zeta zeros can appear as pseudo-Laplacian eigenvalues (the abstract hides both hypotheses). Also Zhu's barrier theorem, and Bombieri 2000 Theorem 10 (with finitely many off-line zeros, the number of negative eigenvalues of large truncations equals half the number of exceptions).  5. The historical-improvement metric '1287 orders of magnitude in first-zero error' compares different windows (λ^2 = 13 versus 250) and is numerical, not a record of a fixed quantity. Its certified part is only the zero count of the finite transform of exported coefficients. Present it as a numerics benchmark, not as progress toward RH.  6. The scope statement says the 2024–2026 claims are covered, but it omits Desogus, arXiv 2609.20367v3 (Oct 2026), which explicitly claims RH: 'Hence every non-trivial zero of ζ has real part 1/2' (Theorem 0.1, via odd-channel Weil positivity and Schur induction from a Y = 7 certificate). It is unrefereed and unverified, and should be recorded as an announced RH claim, not as established.  7. The 'bottleneck_type: mixed' label is fair. The next_concrete_step (verify (H2') for S = {∞, 2, 3}, then ball-arithmetic Schur complement) remains sensible as an analytic route, but it should be re-targeted given Zhu's claim: reproduce or refute the λ^2 ≈ 4.95 certificate rigorously, then test ACL's Theorem 8.14 conditioning at that λ against Zhu's numbers.

**Missing records flagged by verifier.** X. Zhu, 'Weil positivity in compact windows: a finite reduction, certified two-sided bounds, and a Landau–Widom decay law', arXiv 2608.24827v2 (Sep 2026, UNREFEREED). Claims Q(f) >= 8.9e-18\|\|f\|\|^2 for supp f ⊆ [-0.8, 0.8] (λ^2 = e^{1.6} ≈ 4.95, prime powers 2, 3, 4), with simple, even ground state certified (Theorem 6.2), odd sector >= 8.2e-15, and enclosure 8.9e-18 <= λ*(0.8) <= 2.27e-17. It gives a barrier theorem (Thm 1.4): one-stroke certificates need frequency T_1 = 2π e^{A_L}, doubly exponential in L. It reports an RH-conditional upper bound λ*(L) <= exp(-L e^L) and retracts its support-2.38 claim. The lower-bound assembly uses non-interval 50-digit mpmath. Needs independent rigorous replay.; E. Bombieri, 'Remarks on Weil's quadratic functional in the theory of prime numbers, I', Rend. Mat. Acc. Lincei (9) 11 (2000) 183-233. Proves the minimum is attained on windows, reproves Yoshida for small support, and gives Theorem 8 (exact count of negative eigenvalues of finite truncations) and Theorem 10 / alternatives (i)-(iii): with finitely many off-line zeros, the number of negative eigenvalues equals half the number of exceptions. Companion: Bombieri, 'A variational approach to the explicit formula', Comm. Pure Appl. Math. 56 (2003) (cited by Suzuki; not opened).; E. Bombieri, P. Garrett, 'Designed pseudo-Laplacians', arXiv 2002.07929 (2020). A self-adjoint extension of the restricted Laplacian on the modular curve has discrete spectrum contained in {s(s-1) : ζ_k(s) = 0}. Corollary 69: assuming RH AND Montgomery's pair-correlation conjecture, at most 94% of zeta zeros can appear. This is a conditional negative result for pseudo-Laplacian Hilbert–Pólya routes (Colin de Verdière lineage).; A. Groskin, 'A finite Guinand–Weil dictionary and archimedean tail order for the truncated Weil quadratic form', arXiv 2607.02828v3 (Aug 2026, unrefereed). Each Galerkin value <v, Qv> is an exact zero sum of a band-limited test function. The omitted archimedean tail is a totally positive Cauchy–Stieltjes increment, giving a two-sided certification rule with budget B_T ~ (2N+1) ρ log T/(π^2 T). This settles the c = 100 'negative eigenvalue' episode.; M. Desogus, 'The Three Gates: A Rooted-Operator Approach to Weil Positivity', arXiv 2609.20367v3 (Oct 2026, UNREFEREED). Explicit RH-proof claim (Theorem 0.1: A_{a,-} ⪰ 0 for all a in the odd logarithmic channel, hence RH). Should be ledgered as an announced, unverified claim.; D. Kastoris, 'Quantitative prolate approximants in the radical of the Weil quadratic form', Zenodo 10.5281/zenodo.22864295 (21 Sep 2026, preprint). The primary source for the first upper bound ε_λ <= C λ^A (1 - χ_4(λ)) and N(λ) = O_s(λ^{4+2s} log λ). Its abstract is now readable and should replace 'not opened'.; Publication-status updates. CCM 'Zeta spectral triples' is EMS Ser. Lect. Math. (2026) 39-76, DOI 10.4171/elm/37/3. Connes' Letter (arXiv 2602.04022) is J. Open Math. Problems 2 (2026) 1-52. CCM 'Zeta zeros and prolate wave operators' (arXiv 2310.18423) is Ann. Funct. Anal. 15 (2024) art. 87. Suzuki's 'Aspects of the screw function' is J. London Math. Soc. 108 (2023) 1448-1487.; A. Connes, 'Trace formula on the adele class space and Weil positivity', Current Developments in Mathematics 1997, 5-64 (DOI 10.4310/cdm.1997.v1997.n1.a1). An intermediate exposition between the 1996 C. R. note and Selecta 1999 (not opened).; Candidate items not verified here: Lagarias, 'The Schrödinger operator with Morse potential on the right half-line' (Commun. Number Theory Phys., 2009); A. Sands, 'Automorphic Hamiltonians, Epstein zeta functions, and Kronecker limit formulas' (arXiv 2208.02082) and M. Manivel (arXiv 2610.22291), which proves the purely discrete spectrum of Sands' automorphic Hamiltonian; Ramis–Richard-Jung–Thomann, C. R. Math. 363 (2025), on new eigenfunctions of the negative Connes–Moscovici prolate spectrum.


## POS: POS: Positivity-type criteria for RH. Covers Li/Keiper–Li coefficients, semi-local Li criteria, Weil positivity on restricted supports, Jensen polynomials, Turán/Laguerre/Pólya-frequency inequalities,

| LED-POS-1 | Largest n for which the Li (Keiper–Li) coefficients λ_n have been computed directly from ζ, with no zero input, under rigorous error control | λ_n > 0 for all 1 ≤ n ≤ 10^5. λ_100000 (Keiper normalization) = 4.6258078240690223...; Li normalization ≈ 4.6258×10^5.: Johansson computes the Keiper–Li coefficients λ_0..λ_n up to n = 100000. The method takes the power series of log ξ(s) = log(−ζ(s)) + log Γ(1+s/2) + log(1−s) − (s/2) log π at s=0 and right-composes it with x/(x−1). ζ is evaluated by Euler–Maclaurin with rigorous error bounds in ball arithmetic (Arb), at a working precision of 1.1n+50 bits, giving about 0.1n accurate bits. Table 2 reports the times 'to determine the signs of λ_n up to n = 1000, 10000 and 100000'. The paper reports λ_100000 = 4.62 | rigorous_computation | F. **Verifier critique.** 1. Attribution errors in the frontier's (c) and in historical_improvement. 'Jensen … 9.36·10^20 (2022) → 9·10^24 (2026)' is wrong. The zbMATH review of the journal version of GORTTW (Adv. Math. 397, 2022) already states d ≤ 9×10^24 for all n ≥ 0, so Ono 2026 only restates it. The shift-threshold chain 'c·e^{d/2} (2022)' is also mis-dated: the published GORTTW has n > c·e^d, and c·e^{d/2} appears only in arXiv v3 (zbMATH; Holland §12). Ono's unshifted bound is d − C√(d log d), not d − C√d log d (Theorem 1 and proof; abstract LaTeX). Fix item (c) and open question 9 to match.  2. The Weil-window 'next_concrete_step' is misdirected. It proposes linear-forms-in-logarithms lower bounds to 'exclude near-alignment' of (t log p) and so push T♯ below T1 at L ≈ 1.19. Zhu §7 already answers this. On the finite t-range the symbol Ψ_L is an explicit finite expression, certifiable directly by adaptive interval evaluation, so Baker-type bounds are unnecessary there. More importantly, the alignments actually happen: Ψ_L itself, not only the envelope, falls below β* by about 2 near t ≈ 1550, with the last dip at t0 ≈ 8.48×10^3. The achievable gain is therefore only T♯ ≈ 1.2×10^4 → 8.5×10^3. Zhu also states what remains, which is computational scale: N ≈ 1.4–2×10^4 Legendre orders at about 90 digits for λ* ≈ 10^−47, roughly 1000× his 950×950 run, and not carried out. The realistic next step is a compiled multiprecision assembly with an interval certificate of min Ψ_L on [8.5×10^3, 1.2×10^4], plus an independent Arb replay of the L = 0.8 certificate. Diophantine control may matter for asymptotic cost in L, but Zhu's Remark 1.6 frames that as the arithmetic heart of the problem, not a near-term step.  3. Breakthrough item (2) and open question 3 mislabel Brown's theorems. Brown's flawed Theorem 2 (Palojärvi, citing Droll) is the zero-free-region ⟹ λn ≥ 0 direction, which is the Oesterlé direction. The direction 'λ1..λN ≥ 0 ⟹ zero-free region' is Brown's Theorem 3, which is not disputed and is made explicit in Palojärvi's Thm 3.1 (Albanian J. Math. 2020). The proposed target 'λ1..λN ≥ 0 ⟹ no off-line zeros with \|γ\| ≤ c√N' is also too strong. By Voros's (heuristic) uncertainty bound (17), a zero 1/2 + t + iT influences λn only for n ≳ T²/\|t\|. Finitely many positive λn can therefore exclude only zeros at distance \|t\| ≳ T²/N from the line, which is the \|ρ/(ρ−τ)\| < R shape Brown and Palojärvi obtain. They cannot verify RH to height c√N.  4. Several negative results are overstated or mis-typed. (5) Voros n ≳ T²/\|t\| is an uncertainty-principle heuristic, not a theorem; only Oesterlé's unpublished (13) makes the n ≤ T0² part rigorous. (9) Bombieri's theorem says that for large enough truncation the number of negative eigenvalues equals half the number of off-line zeros. It does not show violations are invisible at small truncation, so calling it a negative result is a stretch. (10) 'Any criterion that ignores the Euler product (Jensen/Hermite universality, Toeplitz tails) cannot prove RH' conflates regimes. The full Jensen, Toeplitz and Laguerre criteria (all d, n; all r, k) are equivalent to RH and so implicitly use everything about ξ. Only their asymptotic regimes (large shift, k ≫ r³, Ki cosine limit) are universal across functions that violate RH. Farmer's X10 makes a sharper point than Kim–Lee: all classical J^{d,n} with n ≥ 1 are real-rooted for X10 even though it violates the analogue of RH.  5. The 'bottleneck' claim that every RH-critical regime 'requires controlling cancellation in the prime comb Σ Λ(n) n^{−1/2} ĝ(log n) uniformly in frequency' is accurate for Weil windows (Zhu Thm 1.4, Lemma 3.2). For the Jensen, Toeplitz, Laguerre and Nyman–Beurling regimes it is an unproved unifying conjecture. Those criteria involve Taylor or moment data of Φ (theta) or Möbius-weighted Dirichlet polynomials, and no reduction to the prime comb is given. The structural three-regime classification (archimedean-only; corollaries of finite zero verification scaling like T² or πT; asymptotic tails) is otherwise a fair summary and matches the authors' own scope remarks (Michalowski Remark 1.2, Sra, Ono Remark 4, GORTTW Remark 3).  6. The 'best_established' list leaves out the main unconditional theorem behind the Connes programme. Connes–van Suijlekom (Commun. Math. Phys. 2025) prove that a simple, even ground state of the Galerkin-truncated Weil form has a Mellin transform with only real zeros (ACLG Theorem 7.1 = their [8]). It also omits Suzuki 2026 Theorem 1.4: positivity and simplicity of λ_a for small a, with explicit asymptotics. For (a), note that Connes–Consani's positivity needs vanishing conditions (ĝ(±i/2) = 0, and ĝ(0) = 0 or the c\|ĝ(0)\|² term with 13 < c < 17), while Zhu's Q(f) carries the pole terms explicitly. The half-widths ((log 2)/2 vs 0.8) are comparable, but the statements are not literally the same functional class.  7. Minor points. The history row 'λ² = 13 (primes < 13)' should credit Connes–Consani–Moscovici, 'Zeta spectral triples' (arXiv:2511.22755, 2025) and use primes ≤ 13 (ACLG lists 2, …, 13). Connes's own abstract says 'less than 13'. The Li-coefficient frontier is otherwise accurate: no rigorous direct computation beyond n = 10^5 was found, and the Arb paper (arXiv:1611.02831) restates 10^5. The Weil, PF and Jensen 2026 items are correctly flagged as unrefereed. The 'Λ ≤ 0.13963' item depends on an unverified 7/8 zero-free claim and should not appear among 'possible ingredients' as if established.

**Missing records flagged by verifier.** Connes–van Suijlekom, 'Quadratic forms, real zeros and echoes of the spectral action', Commun. Math. Phys. (2025). Unconditional theorem: if the lowest eigenvalue of the truncated Weil form is simple and its eigenvector is even, the Mellin transform has only real zeros (Carathéodory–Fejér extension). This is the source of ACLG Theorem 7.1 and of the CCM zeta spectral triples; it should be its own record.; Connes–Consani–Moscovici, 'Zeta spectral triples', arXiv:2511.22755 (Nov 2025). First λ² = 13 (primes ≤ 13) computation reproducing 50 zeros, the first to about 10^−55. It is the primary source for the POS-08 history row now credited to Connes 2026.; Suzuki, arXiv:2606.09096, Theorem 1.4. For small a, λ_a > 0 and simple, with λ_a = log(1/a) + μ1 − log 2π + ψ(2) − 1 + O(a): a quantitative small-window positivity record. Also Suzuki, arXiv:2610.32704 (Oct 2026, conditional on RH): eigenvalues of the localized extensions converge to the zeta ordinates.; Danesh, arXiv:2611.10726 (Nov 2026, unrefereed). Quantitative two-interval Weil criterion: RH ⟺ Cauchy–Schwarz for pairs of narrow, widely separated test functions. It includes a short-support positivity estimate and exponential detection of an off-line zero.; Biane–Pitman–Yor, Bull. AMS 38 (2001) 435–465, §2.3. Published secondary mention of Oesterlé's semi-local Li result (Voros's ref. [6]); it should be cited in POS-02.; Generalized and semi-local Li criteria: Freitas, arXiv:math/0507368 (Li-type criterion for zero-free strips); Sekatskii, Ukr. Math. J. 66 (2014), arXiv:1304.7895 and 1404.7276 (generalized Li sums with partial positivity for m ≤ n, b > b_n); Bucur–Ernvall-Hytönen–Odžak–Smajlović (τ-Li numerics for functions violating RH, cited by Palojärvi).; McPhedran–Scott–Maignan, ACM Commun. Comput. Algebra 57 (2023) 85–110: first 4000 Keiper–Li coefficients via a positive representation. This is the primary source for the POS-01 caveat. Also Voros, arXiv:2204.01036 (2022): closed-form modified Keiper–Li sequence tested on Davenport–Heilbronn functions.; Suzuki, arXiv:2301.05779: RH ⟺ every Li coefficient is the norm of an explicit function in a model space (a Weil-type reformulation of Li positivity).; Coffey, Math. Phys. Anal. Geom. 8 (2005), arXiv:math-ph/0505052 ('Toward verification of the Riemann hypothesis: application of the Li criterion'): bounds on the archimedean and arithmetic parts of λn. Also Bombieri–Lagarias, J. Number Theory 77 (1999) (arithmetic formula λn = S∞ − Sf + 1), which deserves a record, not only a history line.; O'Sullivan, arXiv:2007.13582 ('Zeros of Jensen polynomials and asymptotics for the Riemann xi function'): a Hermite-combination variant of the Jensen–Pólya criterion with detailed asymptotics, cited in GORTTW Remark (4) and by Holland.; Published de Bruijn–Newman upper bounds as verified records (neighbouring area, used in POS-15): Polymath Λ ≤ 0.22 (Res. Math. Sci. 6 (2019), arXiv:1904.12438); Platt–Trudgian Λ ≤ 0.2 (arXiv:2004.09765 §3.4); Ki–Kim–Lee Λ < 1/2 (Forum Math. 2009). Also Pólya 1927 as the source of the universal-factor monotonicity.; Katkova, CMFT 7 (2007) 13–31: ξ1 ∈ PF43 (published; the arXiv version says PF44). Also Theorems 2–3 (ξ1 ∈ APF_m for all m; e^{nz}ξ1 ∈ PF_m for large n), which are fixed-order total-positivity records currently mentioned only in passing.

**Verifier critique.** The best_established list is correct item by item, with two venue updates. CMR (the Fourier-optimization bounds on the average of F) is refereed: Math. Comp. 94 (2025). IKT (the MO limits 0.508/3.022) is refereed: Math. Proc. Camb. Phil. Soc. 181 (2026). Every 2026 record still lacks a journal reference on arXiv. All the cited unrefereed values check out, and nothing newer was found up to 2026-11-16 that supersedes mu <= 1/2, lambda > 3.63846723, D(lambda) > 0 for lambda >= 0.7892, or Knausgard's 0.83900 and 0.67354. Main problems:  (1) Bottleneck (1) overstates Lagarias-Rodgers. Their Theorem 3.1 shows AH-consistency only for averaged band-limited n-level correlations of the zeros. It does not cover resonance-weighted statistics, where zero counts are weighted by \|R(t)\|^2 for a Dirichlet polynomial R of length up to T. Those mix prime-side and zero-side information. No theorem says the Bondarenko-Heap quotient-resonance or DPC moment methods saturate at 1/2. The only proved method limits are for MO: CGG 1/2, GTT 0.5042 for y = T^{1-delta}, IKT 0.508 for L <= T. Step 1 of next_concrete_step itself treats this as open.  (2) 'Equivalently, one would need F(alpha) for alpha > 1' is wrong. Knowing F beyond 1 is one sufficient route. Long resonators with exceptional-character coefficients are a different route: Bondarenko-Heap (2608.07399) cross 1/2 under RH plus Siegel zeros without any F(alpha) information. That paper also changes the barrier picture. The classical AH source, Siegel zeros, is not an obstruction once polynomials longer than T are allowed. The real obstruction is the Chowla/Elliott-type shifted correlations of a Mobius-like f.  (3) Simple zeros. Note that 19/27 (RH, mollifier) already exceeds the unconditional bandwidth-one ceiling of about 0.6818, so mollifier methods carry information beyond pair correlation. The unconditional pair-correlation side is now close to the RH pair-correlation side. Knausgard's 0.6780 for simple zeros anywhere is close to CGdL's 0.6792 under RH. The ceiling is AF's definition: certificates that use the first two trace moments against support-[-1,1] test functions plus the on-line/off-line partition. Its numeric enclosure (EnclOK) is not kernel-checked. Knausgard proves his own method limit of 0.67357.  (4) Negative results. Item (f) misquotes Bui-Milinovich, who say only that their result supersedes the Mueller-method results; no limitation of Mueller's method is proved. A negative result is missing: Montgomery-Taylor is optimal among band-limited [-1,1] auxiliary functions (CCLM 2017, Cor. 14; also CMR Sect. 4). CGdL beat it only by moving to the Cohn-Elkies class. Under (c), Conrey-Iwaniec actually require many small gaps on the critical line in a q-dependent height range, and they apply to Hecke L-functions; zeta comes in as a factor of the Dedekind zeta function.  (5) historical_improvement arithmetic. '+1.66 over 37 years' for lambda does not match its own endpoints. 1.9 (1981) to 3.638 (2026) is +1.74 over 45 years; 1.9799 (1984) to 3.638 is +1.66 over 42 years. The unconditional distinct-zero baseline (Wu 2015, 0.6603, versus Ki-Lee 2012, more than 70%) is unresolved and should be marked so in the chain.  (6) next_concrete_step Step 2 is flawed as written. A fixed real character has chi(p) = -1 on only half the primes, so the prime-sum gain that Bondarenko-Heap extract from Siegel-type bias averages to zero at leading order. The proposed density 1 - 2^{-k} 'from k independent characters' is not achievable by any Dirichlet character, because a product of characters is again a character with density 1/2. The multiplicative f with f(p) = -1 off the set where all chi_i(p) = 1 is not periodic, so sum f(n)f(n+h) is an Elliott/Chowla problem, which is exactly the obstacle Bondarenko-Heap name. Periodicity and GRH therefore give nothing beyond density 1/2, and the step needs restating.  (7) Step 1 needs restating too. The Bondarenko-Heap and DPC functionals are defined through Dirichlet coefficients. To 'evaluate them on the AH process', first translate them through the explicit formula into zero-side correlation functionals. With resonator length T^{1/(2c)} the relevant Fourier support is about 1/c < 2 for c > 1/2. That is inside the Lagarias-Rodgers band, which is consistent with both methods stopping exactly at 1/2 and makes the proposed lemma plausible.  (8) Step 3 is partly moot for CGdL, whose constants are already interval-certified by the authors. For DPC the load-bearing piece is the truncation bound (8.3): the error 1.07e-9 consumes about half of the raw margin 2.15e-9.  (9) The bottleneck section omits two whole quantity families that are moving in 2026: positive proportions of large gaps and r-gaps, and isolated zeros plus consecutive moderate gaps. See missing_records.

**Missing records flagged by verifier.** Positive proportion of LARGE gaps (lambda~ with D+(lambda) < 1). RH: Wu, PAMS 142 (2014): lambda~ > 1.6989, refining CGGGHB 1985 and Soundararajan 1996 (as quoted by Zhao 2603.17334). Unconditional: Zhao 2603.17334v2, Theorem 2: lambda~ >= 1 + 2.36e-6; explicit D+(1 + 1e-6, 1) < 1 - 7.5e-104. Earlier: Simonic-Trudgian-Turnage-Butterbaugh, Trans. AMS 375 (2022): 1 + 10^{-10^13}.; r-gaps (Selberg's announced theorem lambda_r >= 1 + Theta/r^alpha, mu_r <= 1 - vartheta/r^alpha, with alpha = 1/2 under RH and 2/3 unconditionally). Conrey-Turnage-Butterbaugh 2018 (RH: Theta = 0.5742, vartheta = 0.2998). Inoue 2024 (complete proof, including the unconditional part; RH constants 0.9064 and 0.4846). Inoue-Kobayashi-Toma, MPCPS 181 (2026): Theta = vartheta = sqrt(2) + o(1) as r -> infinity. Zhao 2603.17334: positive proportions, unconditional constant 2.36e-6, RH constants 0.2160 and 0.1638.; Montgomery pair-count small gaps N(beta,T) >> N(T) (pairs, not consecutive gaps). Montgomery: 0.68, then 0.6695. GGOS 2000: 0.6072 (RH), 0.5781 (GRH). CCLM 2017: 0.6068. CGdL 2020, Theorem 4: 0.6039 (RH + N* ~ N), 0.5769 (GRH + N* ~ N).; Isolated zeros and consecutive moderate gaps, all unconditional. Lamzouri 2610.26841v2: at least 13/20 of zeros are c-isolated for some c > 0 (so simple and on the line); more than 1/2 for c = 1/6; positive proportions of k = 3, 4, 5 consecutive moderate gaps. Angon-Ross-Tang-Zhao 2610.22927v2: for 0 < c <= 1/4, two, three and four consecutive gaps >= 2pi c/log T have densities >= 0.3450014, 0.1812517 and 0.0175021. Gonek-Sahay 2412.15481 posed the question.; 'Simple or on the critical line', and the average of the two proportions, unconditional. Lamzouri 2609.02882: 0.88762 and 0.83625. Cobeli-Zaharescu 2610.23467: more than 8/9. Knausgard 2610.08965: Theorem 1.5 gives 0.88931500; Theorem 1.4 gives 0.83676910 for the average.; Dirichlet-family averaged simple zeros (CLLR family). GRH: Ozluk and Chandee-Lee-Liu-Radziwill 91.66%; Sono 93.22%; CGdL 2020 Theorem 5: 0.9350. Unconditional family averages: Alpoge-Furman Remark 7.2, 0.811 simple and on the line and 0.905 distinct; details omitted and not formalized.; Box and PCC-without-RH conditional results. Baluyot-Goldston-Suriajaya-Turnage-Butterbaugh 2501.14545v3 (under review since 2026-06-10): if all zeros with T < gamma <= 2T lie in a box of width 0.3185/log T, at least 2/3 are simple and on the line. Goldston-Suriajaya 2511.20059 and 2603.28104 (box width b(T) -> 0). Goldston-Lee-Schettler-Suriajaya 2503.15449 (PCC alone, without RH, implies 100% simple and on the line) and 2507.06823 (AH-type PCC gives the same). Nguyen 2611.20347 (Artin L-function analogue).; Negative result: optimality of the Montgomery-Taylor constant C_MT = 1/2 + 2^{-1/2} cot(2^{-1/2}) = 1.32749 among band-limited [-1,1] auxiliary functions (Carneiro-Chandee-Littmann-Milinovich, J. reine angew. Math. 725 (2017), Cor. 14). Pair correlation with band-limited test functions cannot beat 0.6725 simple zeros under RH; CGdL go beyond it only through the Cohn-Elkies class.; Zeros of xi' (adjacent quantity). RH: CGdL Theorem 6 and Cor. 7, N1* <= 1.1175 N1, simple >= 0.8825, distinct >= 0.9412; Farmer-Gonek-Lee: 85.83% simple. Unconditional: AF Remark 7.1 (0.85838 flat window, 0.86864 quartic window; distinct 0.92919 and 0.93432); Conrey 79.874% simple on the line; Wu 0.86957 on the line.; Rigorous verification height. Platt-Trudgian, Bull. LMS 53 (2021) 792-797: RH up to 3,000,175,332,800, covering 12,363,153,437,138 zeros. Simplicity of all these zeros follows from the sign-change count with Turing's method.; Bober-Ge-Milinovich, 'Biases in gaps between zeros of Dirichlet L-functions' (arXiv 2610.31744, 2026-10-22). A rare (density-zero) family of Dirichlet L-functions has provably unusual value distribution and a numerically observed gap-distribution bias tied to a nonvanishing Gauss-type sum. Relevant to GUE numerics and lower-order terms.; Lagarias-Rodgers, 'Band-limited mimicry of point processes by point processes supported on a lattice', Ann. Appl. Probab. 31 (2021) 351-376 (arXiv 1907.03391). Companion to NEG-01; gives the parameter ranges for lattice mimicry of the sine process.; Independent human exposition of the Alpoge-Furman argument: J. Arias de Reyna, arXiv 2610.24612 (2026-10-17). Relevant to the verification status of SPACING-SIMP-03.; Baluyot, J. Number Theory 169 (2016) 183-226: AH formulation allowing multiple zeros, under RH. BGST 2508.10857: density constraints at k/2 under RH + AH, and a stronger AH implies the Essential Simplicity Hypothesis. Both are cited only in passing; they deserve a record under the AH structure.

For sufficiently large T, a positive proportion of the Gram intervals (g_n, g_{n+1}] in [T,2T] contain no zero of zeta(1/2+it). Hence Gram's law (exactly one zero per Gram interval) and the Weak Gram law (at least one zero) both fail for a positive proportion of intervals. A positive proportion of Gram intervals in [T,2T] contain at least one zero, so the Weak Gram law succeeds a positive proportion of the time. **Verifier critique.** Overall the zeta-height frontier is accurate. I found no rigorous H above 3,000,175,332,800 to 2026-11-17 (arXiv, web, zbMATH). The bottleneck is correctly called linear-in-H cost plus rigor overhead. Specific problems follow.  (1) The '725x' factor is Platt-Trudgian's own crude GHz-hour comparison across a 2003 Pentium 4 and a 2020 Xeon. Platt-Trudgian themselves split it between the sampling rate (25 versus 1.2 per zero, about 21x) and multiprecision numerics, leaving at most about 35x attributable to arithmetic. The claim in external-ingredients (b) that 'much of the 725x rigor overhead is avoidable' because Platt's GRH run used SSE2 double intervals is unsupported. That run worked at t up to about 1e8/q. At t about 1e13, the phase t log n alone needs about 48 bits before the binary point, so 53-bit interval arithmetic leaves essentially no fractional precision. The proposed 'certification pass in IEEE double-interval arithmetic' and the 'lemma to prove first' (53-bit analogue of Platt Appendix A) must therefore specify exact or double-double phase reduction. As written, the lemma is likely false.  (2) Negative result (2) is out of date. An unrefereed preprint (Amberger, arXiv 2512.23064, Jan 2026) claims (1.680, 0.186, 0.0314) for t>=653, with c down to 0.018. That goes below the 0.0353 floor of Trudgian's 2011 method by a different technique. It also claims that the Lehman-Brent-Trudgian Gram-block count K is off by a factor of 2: 9 Rosser blocks are needed at heights <=3.6e12, not 6. Neither claim is refereed, but 'about 6 at 2pi*1e12' should not be presented as settled. The main verifications (Platt 2017 and Platt-Trudgian 2021, which use the integrated-S bound directly) do not obviously depend on that corollary.  (3) Negative result (1) cites Selberg's Omega_{+-}((log T)^{1/3}(log log T)^{-7/3}), which is correct as quoted by Trudgian and Brent. It has since been improved (Tsang 1986), though the conclusion is unchanged.  (4) Negative result (5): the x<=2.169e25 range is Platt-Trudgian Corollary 1 via Buethe's criterion 4.92 sqrt(x/log x)<=H. That is about H^2 log x, not 'a constant times H^2'. Johnston (2109.02249) extends the pi(x)-li(x) bound to x<=1.101e26.  (5) The history line should say Brent 1979 reached H(81,000,001) (added in proof), not 75,000,001.  (6) The GRH frontier omits Platt-Trudgian arXiv 2610.29018 (Oct 2026, unrefereed). It gives GRH to height 32 for all real even primitive chi with q<=1e9, no real zeros sigma>0 for all real primitive chi with q<=1e9, and none in [0,1] for odd q<=1e10. This is the biggest real-character advance since Watkins and subsumes most of the Lu-Zaman-Zhao range. The frontier should also flag the unrefereed October 2026 Landau-Siegel exclusion claims (OpenAI, Banks, Kerr, Kimura's Lean-checked constant). If they hold, they change why near-1 real-zero computations matter: the computations then serve only to sharpen constants.  (7) The '(Hiary 4/13 vs zbMATH 3/14)' open question is resolved: the review itself states 4/13 first. The Gourdon-versus-Brent first-Rosser-violation question is resolved in favour of Brent's Table 3, which I viewed directly.  (8) The de Bruijn-Newman remarks should say the only refereed upper bound is 0.2. There is now a swarm of unrefereed 2026 claims (Planat-Raymond Table 1), so citing one (0.156821) without the others is misleading.  (9) The 'possible external ingredients' list should add Platt's ball-arithmetic 'lfunctions' package, the generic certified engine behind the 2026 quadratic-character run, and Tao's project to collect 'primary estimates' such as H and GRH(q,T), which Platt-Trudgian 2026 mention.  (10) Higher-degree caveat: Palojarvi-Zhao's correction propagates to Booker-Then 2018 Theorem 7.1, so the Maass-form verification inherits a (probably harmless) uncorrected Turing bound. This should be listed beside the Booker 2006 remark.

**Missing records flagged by verifier.** Platt, Trudgian, 'Zeros of quadratic Dirichlet L-functions', arXiv 2610.29018 (v1 21 Oct 2026, unrefereed). Theorem 2: for real, even, primitive chi with q<=1e9 (303,963,559 characters), all non-trivial zeros up to height 32 are simple and on the line; about 1.12e6 core hours, Platt 'lfunctions' ball arithmetic. Theorem 3: no zero in [0,1] for real, odd, primitive chi with q<=1e10; Watkins-type sieve in double-interval arithmetic plus lfunctions, about 237,000 core hours. Theorem 1: no real zero s>0 for any real primitive chi with q<=1e9.; Amberger, 'Bounding the integral of the argument of the Riemann zeta function', arXiv 2512.23064 (v3 4 Jan 2026, unrefereed). Theorem 2.1: \|S1(t2)-S1(t1)\| <= 1.680+0.186 loglog t2+0.0314 log t2 for t2>t1>=653; Remark 5.1 gives c=0.018 with a=3.355, b=0.160. Theorem 2.3 claims to correct a factor-2 error in the Lehman-Brent (and Trudgian Corollary 2.3) Gram-block count, needing 9 Rosser blocks at heights <=3.6e12.; Brent 1979 'Added in Proof' (Math. Comp. 33, p.1371): H(81,000,001) by 1 June 1979, with three further Rosser-rule exceptions. Same page: a local window at n=1e10, in which rho_n..rho_{n+10000} are simple and on the line (8622 Gram blocks all satisfying Rosser's rule), and a close pair at n=41,820,581 with gap <0.00053.; Unrefereed October 2026 claims excluding Landau-Siegel zeros, relevant context for VERIF-18 and the GRH frontier. An OpenAI-released manuscript claims (1-beta) log q >= c (absolute, inexplicit). Banks, arXiv 2610.15483, makes c=3.0e-3 for q>=1e10 explicit, and for all q>=3 combined with Lu-Zaman-Zhao. Kimura, arXiv 2610.21511, gives a Lean-checked 5.8e-4 and uses it to list imaginary quadratic fields of class number <=2000 unconditionally. Platt-Trudgian 2026 independently confirm the 16,453,493 count.; Platt 2016's Dirichlet Turing constants (2.17618, 0.0679955), supplied by Trudgian by personal communication and optimized for qt0 about 1e8. These are the constants actually used in the q<=4e5 GRH run; Trudgian's published Theorem 3.3 is optimized for Q=100, t2=2500.; Trudgian 2011 Theorem 4.3: an improved Dedekind-zeta Turing bound, 0.264+1.843N+0.105 log(\|D_K\|(t2/2pi)^N) for t2>t1>40.; Watkins 2004 (Math. Comp. 73, 415-423): no real zeros sigma>0 for real odd chi with q<=3e8. Currently appears only as history inside VERIF-18; it deserves its own record because it was the real-zero record until Platt-Trudgian 2026.; Rigorous Maass-eigenvalue inputs underlying the higher-degree verifications: Booker-Stroembergsson-Venkatesh (rigorous first 10 forms) and Bian's extension to 2191 forms. The completeness of the r<=178 list (versus Booker-Platt's certified r<=177.75) needs a source.; Gourdon's two inconsistent figures for Odlyzko's 2002 dataset at height 10^23: 20 billion (p.3) and 50 billion (p.25). This should be recorded alongside Hiary-Odlyzko's 15-16 billion and Bober-Hiary's 20 billion.
