**Verifier critique.** 1) Major omission: the 2026 quasi-RH claims. The ledger never mentions OpenAI's AI-generated manuscripts (released 6 Oct 2026), which claim no zeros of ζ or of any Dirichlet L-function in Re s > 7/8; the 7/8 paper has a Lean formalization claimed by the release, and there is an 11/12 companion that Quanta reports experts 'largely agree' is correct. Nor does it mention the follow-ups down to 0.874462 (Chang–Wang 2611.00924, conditional on cited inputs) and Ji's 599/600 over Q(i). Downstream papers already use them: Fiori–Jaskari 2609.23222, Ye 2610.15989, Pintz–Althöfer 2610.15559. - All of these are unrefereed. If correct, N(σ,T) = 0 for σ > 7/8. That supersedes every ZDE-04 piece from 7/8 up, ZDE-07, the near-1 parts of ZDE-15/16/17, and Bellotti's PNT error term, and the companion Landau–Siegel exclusion claims would lift the CGL caveat in ZDE-11. - They do not touch sup A (at 7/10), the DH threshold 25/32, or 17/30, so the stated bottleneck survives. But 'best_established' must carry a clearly separated 'claimed, unrefereed' overlay.  2) The bottleneck analysis is arithmetically right but incomplete, and in one respect it points the wrong way. - Incomplete: at σ = 7/10, T = N^{6/5}, three terms of GM (12.1) are tangent to the MVT (all equal N^{0.8}), not two: T^{4/3}N^{2−4σ}, T^{1/2}N^{3−4σ} and T·N^{9/2−7σ}. GM's own remark after (12.1) says T^{4/3}N^{2−4σ} and T^{1/2}N^{3−4σ} are the most important terms and that T = N^{6/5} is chosen to balance them. The ledger's reason ('T^{4/3} is worse for larger T') is only half of that. - Wrong direction: the proposed lever, improving the \|W\|^3N^{1−2σ} energy term (open question 3; δ2 in next step 1), is blocked by Cauchy–Schwarz.   - For any 1-separated W in an interval of length T, E(W) ≥ \|W\|^4/(2T+1). At \|W\| ≈ T·N^{1−2σ} this floor equals \|W\|^3N^{1−2σ}.   - Even feeding the optimal E = \|W\|^4/T into Prop 10.1's term T·N·\|W\|^{1/2}E^{1/2} gives \|W\| ≲ T·N^{8−12σ}, which is exactly the MVT at σ = 7/10 for every T.   - So at 7/10 the binding object is the shape of the S3 bound (Prop 10.1 / Prop 7.2 / §9), not the energy estimate. GM's §13.1 Remark says this explicitly: S1 and S2 are optimal, improvement must come from S3, E(W) sits at the random-set level, and the §9 argument is essentially tight.   - The ledger calls its account 'my own reading' and never cites this remark. Next step 1 should parametrize savings in Prop 10.1's second term or in the §7–9 S3 expansion, not δ2.  3) I re-derived the heuristic numbers under Heuristic 54 with LV ≤ max(2−2σ, 3−4σ+τ(1/2+κ)) on τ ≤ 4/3. With κ = 0 I get sup A = 9/4 at σ = 2/3 and θ = 5/9, and the threshold for beating 30/13 at 7/10 is κ < 1/26, as the ledger states. Both remain heuristics.  4) Historical lines. - The sup-A sequence '4 → 3 → 12/5' is wrong. It should be 4 (Carlson) → about 8/3 (Ingham 1937) → 5/2 (Montgomery 1969) → 12/5 (Huxley) → 30/13, consistent with the θ sequence 5/8 → 3/5 → 7/12 → 17/30. - The DH-threshold start '4/5 (Montgomery 1969)' is doubtful; Montgomery's 1969 bound is A ≤ 2/σ. - The CGL family history has wrong years for Jutila (1972) and Forti–Viola (1973). - 'TTY exhaustive machine optimisation' overstates what TTY did: they optimised over the ANTEDB's recorded inputs.  5) Negative results. - Bourgain's implication is ℓ^q LV conjecture ⇒ Kakeya. DH itself is not shown to imply Kakeya. - Mao–Wein's low-degree failure is unconditional; only the polynomial-time hardness is conditional. - Missing: the Cauchy–Schwarz energy floor and GM's own tightness remark (item 2).  6) The 'ineffective' label on GM is wrong; the result is effective but not explicit.  7) de Faveri–Pandey (2610.01035) is listed only as a vague external ingredient. It gives new critical-line ζ large-value bounds and improved moments M_k for 4 < k ≤ 12, which are direct inputs to Ivić-type zero-density pieces. Nobody has propagated them yet. Its M6 exponent, 5/4 − 1/60, misses Iwaniec's 6/5 − δ threshold for an improvement at σ = 3/4.

**Missing records flagged by verifier.** Quasi-RH claims (unrefereed): OpenAI AI-generated manuscripts released 2026-10-06 claim ζ and all Dirichlet L-functions have no zeros in Re s > 7/8 (Lean formalization claimed by the release) and in Re s > 11/12 (companion, human-edited). Refinements: Liu 2610.12234 (0.874957), Xu–Sang 2610.15291 (0.8748867), Liu 2610.26835 (0.874887, all number fields), Chang–Wang 2611.00924 (0.874462…, conditional on cited inputs), Ji 2611.16665 (599/600 via Q(i)). If correct, N(σ,T) = 0 for σ > 7/8.; Landau–Siegel exclusion claims (OpenAI 2026; re-proofs by Banks 2610.15483 with explicit constant, Kerr 2610.21099, Guo 2610.30476), all unrefereed. These bear on the q-aspect caveats of ZDE-11.; Fiori–Jaskari 2609.23222: new unconditional explicit PNT error bound, plus a QRH-conditional bound \|ψ(x)−x\| < 0.04934·x^{3/4}log²x + 0.02636·x^{7/8}.; de Faveri–Pandey 2610.01035: first improvement since Hardy–Littlewood on large values \|ζ(1/2+it)\| ≥ T^{1/8} (N(T,V) ≪ T^{24/17}V^{−128/17} for V ∈ [T^{7/60}, T^{5/38}]); M6 ≪ T^{5/4−1/60+ε}; improved M_k for 4 < k ≤ 12. A potential input to the ANTEDB zero-density pipeline.; Gafni–Tao exceptional-set records: μ(17/30) ≤ 7/12 and μ(2/15+Δ) ≤ 1 − 9Δ/13 (Ess. Number Th. 5 (2026)).; Runbo Li 2610.18923, Theorem 3 (unrefereed): primes in [x − x^{0.52−c}, x] for a computable c > 0. Also MPPTW 2610.09567 (GM-downstream): Möbius cancellation in all intervals [X, X+X^{19/35+ε}] and Gowers uniformity of Λ at X^{3/5+ε}.; Explicit KLN-type refinements: Fiori–Kadiri–Swidinsky (JMAA 2023), Cully-Hugill–Johnston 2023 (Lemma 7.1), and the improved KLN constants in Chourasiya–Simonič §6.; Johnston 2411.13791 (zero-density estimates and zero-free regions ⇒ near-optimal PNT error; refines Pintz). It is cited inside ZDE-17 but has no record of its own.; Near-critical-line Selberg-type bounds N(σ,T) ≪ T^{1−θ(2σ−1)}log T: Selberg θ = 1/8, Jutila θ < 1/2, Conrey θ < 4/7 (proof completed later; ANTEDB §11.2). These would improve Ingham only once θ > 2/3.; TTY zero additive-energy exponents A*(σ) (from the same Math. Comp. paper, 'zero additive energy estimates'), used in Gafni–Tao Theorem 1.3.; Blomer–Thorner 2410.17158 (Trans. AMS 2026): zero density near the critical line for GL(m) families. Also Sun–Wang 2412.02416 (GL(3), spectral aspect) and Yin 2610.37371 (sym² L-functions, weight aspect).; Pintz 2408.00617 (unrefereed): DH for σ ≥ 0.9 for degree ≤ 2 Selberg-class functions. It appears only inside a ZDE-12 caveat and deserves its own record.; Starichkova 2411.01845: generalised Ingham-type theorem linking zero-free regions, zero density and PNT in short intervals. Also Pintz 2310.04544, a simple proof of DH-breaking density bounds.; Bellotti–Trudgian–Yang 2603.21490: explicit zero-free region 1 − 1/(4.896 log t), an input to explicit near-1 zero-density bounds.

**Verifier critique.** The classical, Littlewood and VK parts of the frontier are accurate in substance. The quasi-RH part is out of date and its 'barrier' language is too strong.  (1) Stale best claimed exponent. As of 2026-11-17 the smallest claimed uniform boundary is Liu's B_tw = 0.8740585038 (hexagon:2610.00655v3, 3 Nov 2026), for every finite-order Hecke L-function over every number field. It passes through B* = 0.8744683, B' = 0.8742213 and B_ex = 0.8740728. Chang-Wang (arXiv:2611.00924, 0.8744621, conditional) has already been passed. The value 0.874886647 is no longer the frontier.  (2) The 'optimal within the comparison system' results (Liu Theorem 34.4; the B_flat 'exact infimum'; B_ex and B_tw optimality) are not real negative results. They have been bypassed four or more times in about 3 weeks by adding estimates such as amplified numerator moments, exhaustion-aware returns and unit-twisted primes. Negative result (f) and bottleneck (3) should be restated as system-relative facts, not barriers. The structural limit 'rows u = p^6 number about H^{1/6}' belongs to the 11/12 argument only.  (3) Verification framing misses an independent formal artifact. Cureton-Thorner (hexagon:2610.00200v2, 12 Oct 2026) prove zeta_K(s) != 0 for Re s > 31/32 for every number field K. Their proof is formalized in Lean by AxiomProver (github.com/AxiomMath/DedekindZetaZeroFree), and the repository reports a comparator pass. Taking K = Q gives a second formalization, from a different provider, of a fixed zero-free half-plane for zeta. 'Replay the OpenAI comparator' is therefore not the only decisive check: replaying both and cross-checking statement fidelity is the natural next step. Neither replay was done in the ledger or here.  (4) Bottleneck (1) is mis-stated. BTY's R = 4.896 is set by the crossover height with the Littlewood region (Lemma 1 proved to exp(76.47)), not by saturation of the zero detector. The ledger also overlooks that the refereed Ford Theorem 3 + Hiary-Patel-Yang region (Yang 2024 eq. (1.4)) is wider than both BTY and Yang for exp(73.0) to exp(170.2). Using it as the crossover would require a Lemma 1-type certification of R = 4.8594 only up to about exp(75.25), against exp(79.0) with Yang's published 21.233, without relying on the unpublished thesis constant 19.62. This is a concrete, cheap experiment.  (5) Negative result (d) overstates Ford. Ford says 'improving greatly on Stechkin's region for all \|t\| >= 3 with (1.5) is not possible' at T0 = 5.45*10^8. That is a remark about one method at the 2000 verification height, not an impossibility. Kadiri and successors improved the region greatly with smoothing.  (6) Negative result (g) and open question 5 can be partly closed. The v5 Theorem 3 numerics are at least as wide as the published form for log t >= 30, so the MTY and Yang intermediate claims are not undermined. Ford's Theorems 3-5 remain valid because the bounds that replace Cheng-Graham (Lehman's, HPY's) are stronger.  (7) Bottleneck (2) is correct. B = 4.43795 already includes Preobrazhenskii's improvement, so the 'MTY says Preobrazhenskii should lower B' lever is used up.  (8) The pre-2026 statements are fine: 'VK shape unchanged since 1958 in refereed work', the DMV Beurling barrier, Landau's V bounds (34.468305 < V < 34.4889920009), and MTY's 52.74 inductive scheme. One caveat: 52.74 was computed with Ford's B = 4.45 and is not directly comparable to Bellotti's 53.989.  (9) Missing hypothesis scope. Every 2026 quasi-RH follow-up is conditional on the unrefereed OpenAI 7/8 or 11/12 analytic inputs. The all-number-field ones also depend on Cureton-Thorner's general-field Kazhdan-Patterson inputs. None is refereed. The Lean status differs by result: OpenAI 7/8 has a comparator challenge (unreplayed here); Cureton-Thorner 31/32 has a comparator pass reported by its own repository (unreplayed here); Liu's Bnew is Lean-formalized on top of the OAI foundation files; Liu's B_tw is 'in progress'; 11/12 and Xu-Sang have none.  (10) The Pintz exponent formula in ZFR-18 has c^2 where it should have c^3.

Theorem 1.1 claims Re s > B_tw = 0.874058503791859..., the root of a degree-6 polynomial, for all finite-order Hecke L-functions over all number fields, including zeta. The paper also proves B' = 0.874221250 (Thm 1.3) and B_ex = 0.874072847666 (Thm 1.2, quartic), with optimality claimed within its system.
